I just watch what I put in my body. That's all. \n\nIf I eat heavy stuff—like rice, or bread, or anything greasy—I just... I can't stop thinking about how it's gonna sit on me right away. Make me slow. So I keep an eye on it. I'll usually check the scale or see how my belt fits the next morning, just to make sure I didn't let myself go. I don't see the issue with that.\n\nDoctor: After eating, do you ever make yourself vomit or do anything else to try to prevent gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No. I don't... I'm not making myself sick or anything like that. I just work. Like I said, I just watch what I eat.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself being really preoccupied with your body shape or weight—like thinking about it a lot, checking yourself in mirrors, or feeling anxious if you can't check?\n\nPatient: I guess... I mean, I look. Yeah. \n\nWhen I pass a mirror, I'll check my stomach. My waist. Just to see. If it's not... if I don't look tight, or small enough right there... I just feel like I'm losing it. Like I'm not that same disciplined guy I always was on the warehouse floor. I just need to know I'm still sharp. That's it.\n\nDoctor: Does worrying about your weight or body shape ever interfere with your daily work or make you feel really anxious or upset?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I get upset. But... I guess it is interfering, yeah. \n\nI've got this new logistics job lined up. But they... they won't clear me to start. Not until I get this addressed. The weight stuff. I just watch what I put in my body. That's all. \n\nIf I eat heavy stuff—like rice, or bread, or anything greasy—I just... I can't stop thinking about how it's gonna sit on me right away. That's all.\n\nDoctor: When you look at yourself, do you feel like your body looks bigger or heavier than it actually is, even if others might say it's not?\n\nPatient: I don't... no. That's it.\n\nDoctor: Does worrying about your weight or body shape ever interfere with your daily work or make you feel really anxious or upset?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I get upset. But... I guess it is interfering, yeah. \n\nI've got this new logistics job lined up. But they... they won't clear me to start. Not until I get this addressed. The weight stuff. So my work plans are just... on hold right now. Even though I keep telling myself I'm ready to go, I'm job-ready. But... yeah. It's holding me up.\n\nDoctor: Do you have a fear of gaining weight or becoming fat, even though you know you might already be underweight?\n\nPatient: I don't know about the word fear. That's... I wouldn't call it that. But I guess I don't want to get heavy. \n\nIf I eat certain things... like rice, or bread. Or something greasy. I just... I can't stop thinking it's going to sit on me right away. So I'll usually check the scale. Or see how my belt fits the next morning. Just to make sure I didn't let myself go. I just like to keep on top of it, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're overweight or not thin enough, even when others might say you're already underweight or at a healthy weight?\n\nPatient: I don't... I mean, people say things. My sister, and my regular doctor... they keep saying I'm in danger. That I'm too small. \n\nBut... I think they're just making it a bigger deal than it really is. They don't understand. I'm just trying to keep myself light. Sharp. Ready for the floor. I'm not trying to be... whatever they think. Just ready to work.\n\nDoctor: When you think about yourself and how you judge who you are, how much does your weight or body shape influence that judgment?\n\nPatient: I don't know if it's about judging who I am. That sounds a little... I don't know. \n\nBut... I guess when I look in the mirror... mostly at my stomach, or my waist... if it doesn't look tight, or smaller... I just feel like I'm not that same disciplined guy I used to be on the warehouse floor. It just makes me feel like I'm losing my edge. That's all.\n\nDoctor: When you look at yourself, do you feel like your body looks bigger or heavier than it actually is, even if others might say it's not?\n\nPatient: I don't... no. I'm not seeing things that aren't there. \n\nI know I'm not a big guy. I just look in the mirror and... I see what's there. I'm not imagining anything. I just want to make sure it stays the way it is. Just ready to work.\n\nDoctor: When you think about yourself and how you judge who you are, how much does your weight or body shape influence that judgment?\n\nPatient: I don't know if it's about judging who I am. That sounds a little... I don't know. \n\nBut... I guess when I look in the mirror... mostly at my stomach, or my waist... if it doesn't look tight, or smaller... I just feel like I'm not that same disciplined guy I used to be on the warehouse floor. It just makes me feel like I'm losing my edge. That's all.\n\nDoctor: When you look at yourself, do you feel like your body looks bigger or heavier than it actually is, even if others might say it's not?\n\nPatient: I don't... no. I'm not seeing things that aren't there. \n\nI know I'm not a big guy. I just look in the mirror and... I see what's there. I'm not imagining anything. I just want to make sure it stays the way it is. That's it.\n\nDoctor: When you look at yourself, do you ever feel like certain parts of your body, like your stomach or waist, appear larger to you than they actually are—even if others might not see it that way?\n\nPatient: No. I don't... I don't think so. \n\nLike I said, I just see what's in front of me. If it's not tight enough, then it's just not. I'm not... making things up in my head to look bigger. I just see it for what it is.\n\nDoctor: When you evaluate how you're doing as a person or feel about yourself, does your weight or how you look play a major role in that evaluation?\n\nPatient: I thought I just... I mean, like I said a minute ago. \n\nIf I look in the mirror and my stomach or my waist isn't tight... if I'm not keeping it small, then I just feel like I've lost my discipline. The kind of discipline I had to have on the warehouse floor. So... yeah, I guess it matters. It's just how I know I'm still keeping it together.\n\nDoctor: When you think about what makes you valuable or successful as a person, does it mostly come down to how disciplined you are about your body and how you look?\n\nPatient: I don't know about words like valuable. But... yeah, it's like I just told you. \n\nWhen I look in the mirror... especially at my stomach and waist... if I'm not smaller, if it's not tight-looking, then I just... I feel like I'm not the disciplined man I used to be on the warehouse floor. That's all it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your weight or the shape of your body, especially your stomach and waist, tells you a lot about whether you're doing well or poorly in life?\n\nPatient: I mean... I feel like we're just going in circles here. \n\nLike I said... if I look in the mirror and my stomach isn't tight, if I'm not keeping the weight off... it just means I'm slipping. It means I don't have that discipline anymore. The same discipline I needed for the warehouse. So... yeah. It tells me if I'm keeping things in line.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that how you see your body, especially your stomach and waist, has a strong impact on how you feel about yourself overall?\n\nPatient: I... look, doc, I don't know how many different ways I can say the same thing. \n\nYeah. It impacts me. Like I've been saying... if I check the mirror and my stomach isn't tight, if I'm not keeping it small... I just feel like I've lost my edge. That discipline I used to have working the floor. So... yeah. It does.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively or push yourself really hard physically to prevent gaining weight or to feel in control of your body?\n\nPatient: No. Not really. My body took enough of a beating all those years on the warehouse floor, so... no. I don't go to a gym or work out or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: When you look at your body, especially your stomach and waist, do you feel that they appear larger or heavier to you than they actually are, even if others might not see it that way?\n\nPatient: I don't... no.
